# Experiment ledger

One row per hypothesis. Register before running; append results, never rewrite. Nothing is
promoted without passing its held-out gate. Receipts are `research.run` ids in the own database
and reports under `.local/reports/` (not committed).

Gates: **G1** replay on tuning sessions; **G2** one replay on held-out sessions (enforced:
`research.held_out_use` allows each strategy family one use of each held-out session, whatever the
version, so a later version needs held-out sessions no earlier version has seen);
**G3** forward PAPER; **G4** LIVE on one lot.

Rules:

1. A strategy change is a new strategy file version, hence a new configuration hash and a new row.
2. Sessions: from ecr-v3 on (user decision 2026-09-25), strategies are evaluated on market data from
   the week of 21 Sep 2026 onward, including each new session as it is captured, and not on older
   data (`evaluation.sessions_from` in the strategy file). The original split (tuning 11–21 Sep,
   held-out 22–25 Sep) applied to A-001; A-002 consumes 22–25 Sep for the early-confirm-runner family,
   so later versions are judged on sessions from 28 Sep onward.
3. Every result reports both fill models (base 250 ms / 0 bp, stressed 1 s / 25 bp) after dated costs,
   and keeps frames (per-minute observations) apart from episodes (traded campaigns).
4. Changing a threshold after seeing held-out results closes the row as CLOSED_FAIL; the new
   threshold gets a new row and needs fresh held-out data.

Rules fixed now, round numbers, before any result: levels = PDH, PDL, previous close, prior-day ORH/ORL, today's ORH/ORL (after 09:30); cross = first minute close through a level that day in that direction, 09:20–14:30; writer confirmation at the cross minute = 15-minute change of Σ put OI at ATM ± 2 strikes (nearest expiry) > 0 and of Σ call OI < 0 for a long (mirror for a short); volume burst = a 1-minute futures volume within 3 minutes before or after the cross ≥ 2 × the median 1-minute volume of the prior 30 minutes; entry at the cross close (or up to 3 minutes later when the burst comes after); stop = level − 25 NIFTY / 80 SENSEX points; target = 2 × the stop distance; flat 15:15; no time stop; one trade at a time per index; never the expiring index | to be built as `level-writer-break.v1.yaml` (SetupStrategy family) if the pre-check warrants | Pre-check on index points (scratchpad wf/levelbreak.py) on the 9 own-capture days, 9 Oct apart, with the unfiltered level-cross and each filter alone reported beside the full rule; then the real replay on the 13 replayed days with the 7 unreplayed archive days (2, 7, 9, 18, 23, 25, 28 Sep) as held-out | Pre-check worth building only if the full rule beats the unfiltered level-cross in average net points on the 16 non-design index-days with ≥ 15 trades. | A-157 | 2026-10-21 | Near-ATM OI bursts over 1 / 3 / 5 minutes, alone and with level breaks and futures volume (user, 21 Oct evening, on the 21 Oct NIFTY OI tape: "consider 1 minute OI changes along with 3 minutes 5 minutes, choose the best case along with and/or level breakouts and/or future volume if the data is supportive"; "around ATM oi changes primarily"). Priors: A-068 (near-ATM flow does not predict the next hour), A-131–A-135 / A-142 (box break + same-minute OI flow + volume FAIL out of sample), A-152 oi-pulse FAIL, A-154 (builds follow price). New here: the window choice and standard levels, chosen on one sample and judged on others. A-151–A-156 are on their research branches. Hypothesis: some combination of a near-ATM OI burst (window 1, 3 or 5 min) with or without a level break and/or high futures volume predicts the index's next 30 minutes in the burst's direction, and the OI part adds to the same rule without it | Event study only, no strategy: `docs/studies/2026-10-21-atm-oi-burst.py` (+ .md), branch research/a157-atm-oi off main 553d783. Definitions (fixed here): per underlying (NIFTY, SENSEX), nearest expiry ≥ the day; at the close of minute m (09:30–14:45 IST), ATM from the index close of m, band ATM ± 2 strikes; share_w = (Δ put OI − Δ call OI over those strikes from m − w to m) / their summed call + put OI at m − w, w ∈ {1, 3, 5}; positive = up. Burst: |share_w| ≥ a threshold ∈ {90th, 95th, 98th percentile of |share_w|, per underlying and w, over all eligible minutes of the selection sample}. Break B (in the burst's direction): the index close crosses ORH (09:15–09:30 high) or PDH upward (ORL / PDL downward) between the close of m − w and the close of m. Volume V: near-month futures volume over minutes m − w + 1 … m ≥ 1.5 × the median of the same minutes over up to 20 previous sessions of the same source (≥ 5 needed). Variants: 3 windows × 3 thresholds × {OI, OI∧B, OI∧V, OI∧B∧V} = 36; baselines without OI: B, V, B∧V per window. Outcome: signed index return in the event's direction from the close of m + 1 to the close of m + 31 (bp); secondary m + 16, and the ATM option in the direction (CE up / PE down, ATM at m + 1) % return over the same 30 min (bars only). One event per underlying, direction and variant per 15 minutes | Registered before any computation. AMENDED 21 Oct ~21:10, before any result was seen (the first run was stopped unread): the stored bars carry option OI only every 3 minutes for NIFTY (124 changes in 375 minutes; SENSEX ~91), so 1-minute windows cannot be measured on bars; the user: "why cant you first focus on the tick days". Selection: own-capture tick days 28, 29, 30 Sep, 1, 5, 6, 7, 8 Oct (60-s NIFTY OI clock, BSE ~162 s). Validation: own-capture 9, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 19 Oct. Bar check (only if the chosen window is 3 or 5 min): bar days 1 Jul–30 Sep less held-out. Held-out 22–25 Sep read once after (bars if w ≥ 3; zt_archive ticks if w = 1). 21 Oct reported apart, never counted. Volume V amended (own capture starts 28 Sep, no 20-session history): futures volume over the window ≥ 1.5 × the median of all same-length windows earlier that day (from 09:15). AMENDED again 21 Oct ~21:25, still before any result (user: "the rule should keep in mind about the 15 minutes OI changes low priority, in some cases where sudden changes happen no need to trust 15 minutes or since opening day but the data should be strong relative to the day, run only this month October tick days"): no 15-minute or since-open OI condition; the burst threshold is relative to the day: |share_w| ≥ k × the mean |share_w| of the same day's earlier minutes from 09:16 (≥ 10 of them), k ∈ {3, 5, 8} (replaces the selection percentiles); October own-capture tick days only: selection 1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 Oct; validation 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 19 Oct; no bar check and no held-out (22–25 Sep are not October); 21 Oct reported apart. Secondary outcome: the ATM option's % return from own-capture last traded prices | Selection rule: among the 36 variants (3 windows × k ∈ {3, 5, 8} × 4 combinations) with ≥ 20 events in selection, the highest mean 30-min signed return. PASS if, for that variant, (1) validation mean > 0 and the 2.5th percentile of a day-bootstrap (10,000) of the mean > 0; (2) validation mean > the mean of its no-OI baseline (same w; B, V or B∧V; for OI alone > 0 is all). FAIL otherwise. Any PASS is thin (12 days) and leads only to a separately registered strategy tested on further days, never a change to a live strategy | Mac, 21 Oct ~22:30 (`.local/a157/main.txt`). Selection (1, 5–9 Oct): every variant near zero; the eligible best was OI∧V, w = 3, k = 3: 33 events, +3.7 bp, hit 52 %; the no-OI baselines B, V, B∧V were all negative (−0.3 to −5.1 bp). Validation (12–19 Oct) for OI∧V w3 k3: 25 events on 6 days (NIFTY 11, SENSEX 14), **+14.6 bp** mean 30-min, hit 84 %, day-bootstrap 95 % CI [+7.9, +24.6]; 15-min +10.3 bp; ATM option +28.6 % on average over the 30 minutes (last traded prices, before costs); no-OI baseline V w3: 184 events, +1.6 bp. Every OI variant was positive in validation (descriptive). 21 Oct (apart): 6 events, all down, all right, +24.2 bp, option +24.2 %. Caveats: selection was flat, so the validation strength is the 12–19 Oct regime (trend days) more than a proven choice; NIFTY and SENSEX events often fire in the same minute (about 15 independent signals, not 25); a fixed 30-minute exit, no stop, no costs | PASS (thin, 12 days, as registered). Not a strategy yet: next is a separately registered strategy with exactly these rules, replayed, then forward days | `docs/studies/2026-10-21-atm-oi-burst.py` (+ .md); `.local/a157/` |

| A-161 | 2026-10-22 | OI climax exit for aob (user, 22 Oct ~00:20: "try the idea if we can", after the 1 Oct review: at the 14:04 low the near-ATM call writing was at its heaviest (+1.12M in 3 min) and flipped only at 14:10). Hypothesis: a burst of the trade's own driving OI (calls written for a put, puts written for a call) at a new extreme of the index since entry marks capitulation; exiting there beats the premium trail alone. Definitions (fixed here): ATM ± 2 strikes (ATM from the index close of minute m), nearest expiry; driving share = Δ call OI over 3 min / the band's OI at m − 3 for a put (Δ put OI for a call); climax at minute m when the driving share ≥ 3 × the mean |driving share| of the day's earlier minutes from 09:16 (≥ 10), and the index's low (put; high for a call) over m − 2 … m is a new extreme since entry, and the trade is ≥ 10 minutes old and the option's last price is above the entry price; exit at the next minute's price. E5a = aob-v2's exits (premium trail +20 % / 15 %, 30 % stop, 15:15) plus the climax exit, whichever first; E5b = the climax exit with the 30 % stop and 15:15 only (descriptive) | Study only: `docs/studies/2026-10-22-aob-climax.py` on the A-157 minute data (own capture, option last traded prices, the A-158 exit study's simulator; entries exactly aob's) | Registered before any computation. Hypothesis: on days whose opening dealer gamma exposure is negative, the index trends more (and moves more) from 09:45 to 15:24 than on positive-gamma days. | A-165 | 2026-10-22 | Futures order-flow (OFI) entry-timing gate (user, 22 Oct: "yes register and test the futures order flow gate"; Cont, Kukanov & Stoikov 2014: over short intervals price changes are driven mainly by order-flow imbalance at the best bid / ask, linearly and more robustly than volume; evidence mostly contemporaneous). Hypothesis: the near-month index future's OFI over the last 30 s predicts its mid move over the next 60 s in the same direction, by enough to matter for an option entry (a gate: buy a call when recent flow is not against it, else wait). Hypothesis: capping the loss at each position's stop at a share of equity, and halving the size of a same-strategy same-direction entry on the other index within 15 minutes, cuts drawdown and the worst day by more than it cuts net P&L (better return per unit of drawdown). Definitions: risk v18x = v17 + `account.max_loss_per_trade_pct` (the premium a single-leg entry may buy is capped so premium × its stop % ≤ x % of equity; equity as v17) for x ∈ {2, 3, 5, 8}, + `sizing.correlated_entry_factor` 0.5 (a strategy's entry on one index within 15 minutes of its same-side entry on the other index gets half its budget). v18c = v17 + the correlation factor only. | A-183 | 2026-10-24 | Impulse scalp with a fixed 10 % / 20 % target (user, 24 Oct, on charts of 29 Sep 11:05–11:34 and 12:33, 5 Oct 09:30–09:57, 7 Oct 13:10, 9 Oct 09:35–10:15, 14 Oct 13:10–13:16: "why the application not traded when there are clear indicators data with a target of 10 % or 20 % ... derive best and honest strategy ... don't assume, do sincere and serious research"). Hypothesis: a sharp index move confirmed by a futures volume surge and by near-ATM option writers on the move's side is followed often enough by a +10 % (or +20 %) move in the ATM option, before its stop, to pay after real costs. Trigger at the close of minute m, 09:20–14:50, per index: (a) |index close m / close m−3 − 1| ≥ 0.20 %, direction = its sign; (b) near-month futures volume over m−2..m ≥ 2 × the median of the day's earlier 3-minute windows; (c) the ATM ± 2 OI share over m−3..m (A-157: Δput − Δcall over the strikes' OI) of the move's sign (a fall needs calls written faster than puts or puts covered). One per index and direction per 15 minutes. Entry: ATM option of the move's side (CE up, PE down), nearest expiry, at m + 1 | `docs/studies/2026-10-24-impulse-scalp.py` (study only) | Registered before any computation. Exits, both reported: E10 = +10 % target / −10 % stop / 20-minute limit; E20 = +20 % / −15 % / 30 minutes. Ticks (own capture): buy at the ask of the first quote at or after m + 1:00, target / stop on the bid, time exit at the bid. Bars: buy at the close of m + 1 + 0.75 %, target if the bar high reaches it, stop if the low does (both in one bar = stop), time exit at the close − 0.75 %. Samples: bars Oct 2024–Dec 2025 (halves to / from Jul 2025) and Jan–Sep 2026 (held-out 22–25 Sep excluded); own tick days 28 Sep–22 Oct (17; they contain the user's example days, so in-sample, stated) and 23 Oct apart. A pass → a strategy (new family) through engine replays, then the user's decision; a fail is reported with what each condition adds | 24 Oct (`.local/a183-*.txt`). | A-185 | 2026-10-24 | Day-extreme break with a volume surge (user, 24 Oct, on 29 Sep 11:34: "use a breakout of the high of the day rule: if the price breaks above a prior peak with an amber volume bar, buy a call" — "lets implement, give me the best plan"). Not tested before (A-176 / A-183 used 3-minute moves, A-082 / A-173 the opening range). Trigger at the close of minute m, 09:45–14:50, per index: (a) the index close above the highest 1-min high of 09:15..m−1 (call) or below the lowest low (put); (b) near-month futures volume of minute m ≥ 3 × the median 1-minute volume of the day's earlier minutes (≥ 5 × reported); (c, variant) the ATM ± 2 OI share over m−3..m on the break's side (A-157). One per index and direction per 30 minutes. Entry, exits and pricing exactly as A-183 (E10 +10 % / −10 % / 20 min; E20 +20 % / −15 % / 30 min; ticks ask in / bid out; bars close + 0.75 % in, high / low, both = stop, 0.75 % out) | `docs/studies/2026-10-24-day-extreme-break.py` (study only; A-183's loaders and exits) | Registered before any computation. | A-189 | 2026-10-24 | Expiry close squeeze (user, 24 Oct: "on expiry day sellers have to close their positions, short covering so the price moves, it is better to catch such moves" → "yes register and test A-189"). From the 8 own expiry days (15:20–15:30, `docs/research/expiry-1520-1530/`): 4 had a sharp move into the close with first / second out-of-the-money options up 3–8 ×, the others reversed; OI fell into the close but too slowly (NIFTY 60 s, SENSEX ~162 s clock) to time it. Note: the index value (live feed and Upstox's historical bars) freezes 15:15–15:28, so spot = synthetic (ATM strike + call − put of the expiring series). Rule, expiring index on its expiry day only: synthetic spot S per minute (bars: closes; ticks: bid/ask mids); at the close of each minute m from 15:20 to 15:27, trigger if S(m)/S(m−3) − 1 ≥ +0.15 % and S(m) > S(15:15) (call), or ≤ −0.15 % and S(m) < S(15:15) (put); first trigger of the day only. Buy the first strike out of the money in that direction (call: the lowest strike above S(m); put: the highest below). Exit A: hold to 15:29 (ticks: the bid at 15:29:30; bars: the 15:29 close); exit B: out when the premium is 30 % below its high since entry (entry = first high), else as A | `docs/studies/2026-10-24-expiry-close-squeeze.py` (study only) | Registered before any computation. Bars: buy at the close of m + 1 + 0.75 %, B checks each bar's low against the high so far before the bar's high updates it, exits − 0.75 %. Ticks: ask in, bid out. Samples: design = expiry days Oct 2024–Sep 2025 (bars), validation = Oct 2025–Sep 2026 (bars; held-out 22–25 Sep excluded — 24 Sep's 15:14–15:29 index bars were looked at once on 24 Oct only to confirm the freeze, stated), final = the 8 own tick expiry days (28 Sep–22 Oct). Descriptive baseline: buy the first OTM call and put at 15:24 and hold to 15:29 | PASS if, for the same exit, mean net % per trade > 0 on design and on validation, the validation bootstrap 95 % CI above 0, and the tick-day mean ≥ 0. A pass → an expiry strategy built in the engine on the synthetic spot (the index feed is frozen after 15:15), then shadow, then the user's decision | 24 Oct (`.local/a189-*.txt`). Design Oct 2024–Sep 2025: 103 expiry days, **0 triggers**; validation Oct 2025–Sep 2026: 102 days, 11 triggers, all in Aug–Sep 2026 — A mean −53.8 % (median −99 %, win 18 %, CI [−99 %, +12 %]), B mean +62.2 % from one +924 % trade (median −30.5 %, win 18 %, CI [−30 %, +238 %]); tick days (8): 6 triggers, A mean −47.1 % (5 of 6 → ~0, one +210 %: 15 Oct SENSEX 74200 CE 37.15 → 115.35), B +5.1 % (median −20 %). Baseline (first OTM call + put at 15:24 to 15:29): design −49.2 %, validation −21.1 %. Why design has no triggers: the synthetic spot's largest move after 15:15 on expiry days was a median 1–4 bp every month from Oct 2024 to Jun 2026 (6 bp Jul 2026), then 30 bp (Aug 2026) and 36 bp (Sep 2026): the expiry close changed character in Aug 2026 (consistent with a closing auction; md.auction_tick has been captured since the start; inference, not verified against the exchange circular). Before that the expiring options were already priced at the 30-minute closing average by 15:15 | FAIL (design has no trades; validation and ticks negative or one-trade) | The late-expiry squeeze exists only since Aug 2026 (~2.5 months, ~23 expiries); the 0.15 % / 3-minute trigger catches it on 15 Oct but most triggers reverse to zero by 15:30. Re-test only when the new regime has ≥ 40 expiries, as a new registration |
| A-190 | 2026-10-24 | Late expiry spike scalp (user, 24 Oct: "try this — buy a first-strike option only once the move starts after 15:24, and exit on a spike"). Designed from the 8 own expiry days (`docs/research/expiry-1520-1530/`: peaks of 3–8 × at 15:26–15:28 on 1, 8, 15, 22 Oct), so the tick days are in-sample, stated; only new-regime expiries count (A-189: the late move exists from Aug 2026). Rule, expiring index on its expiry day: synthetic spot S (ATM strike + call − put, bid/ask mids; bars: closes); between 15:24:00 and 15:28:00, the first time S has moved ≥ 0.10 % in 60 seconds (ticks: checked every second against S 60 s earlier; bars: the 1-minute close-to-close move of minutes 15:24–15:27), buy the first strike out of the money in that direction (call up, put down). Exits: X50 = bid ≥ +50 % → sell, bid ≤ −50 % → sell, else the bid at 15:29:30; X100 = +100 % / −50 % / 15:29:30 | `docs/studies/2026-10-24-expiry-spike-scalp.py` (study only; A-189's loaders) | Registered before any computation. Ticks: ask in, bid out, per second. Bars: buy at the close of the next minute + 0.75 %; target if the high reaches it, stop if the low does (both = stop), else the 15:29 close; 0.75 % out. Samples: the 8 own tick expiry days (in-sample) and the bar expiry days Aug–Sep 2026 not in the tick set and not held-out (22–25 Sep) | PASS if, for the same exit, mean net % > 0 and win rate ≥ 50 % on the tick days and on the bar days. Preliminary by size (~20 trades): even a pass means a re-test on new expiries, no strategy, until the new regime has ≥ 40 expiries | 24 Oct (`.local/a190.txt`; a bar-stop sign bug — a −50 % stop booked as +49 % — was found in the first run and fixed before scoring). Tick days (8, in-sample): 6 triggers; X50 mean −12.3 % median −51.6 % win 33 % (2 targets, 4 stops); X100 mean +7.5 % win 33 %. The −50 % stop was hit by second-level noise before the spike on two of the biggest days (29 Sep 22650 CE stopped at 15:24:47, 17 s after entry, peak later +384 %; 15 Oct 74200 CE stopped 15:26:19, peak later +195 %). Bars Aug–Sep 2026 (13 expiries, 6 triggers): X50 −17.4 % (win 33 %), X100 −25.7 % (win 17 %) | FAIL | Cheap first-OTM options swing more than ± 50 % within seconds in the last minutes; the spikes are real but their timing is not readable from a 60-second move |

Interpretations fixed here (stated to the user): cycle per NIFTY weekly expiry E, starting the first trading day after the previous expiry at the 09:50 bar close; "closest to ₹30" = the strike of that side whose 1-min close is nearest ₹30; adjustment = buy back the leg at that minute's close and sell the same side at the strike nearest 87.5 % of the remaining leg's close; 15:15 "much lower" = below half of the other leg's close → same adjustment; straddle = PE strike ≥ CE strike, baseline = the combined close at that minute, then only the 10 % rule and expiry apply; after a 10 % stop the next cycle is the next weekly expiry from 09:50 on the next trading day; open legs settle at the expiry day's 15:29 close | `docs/studies/2026-10-24-weekly-strangle.py` (study only) | Registered before any computation. 1-minute option bars (hist.candle), closes carried within a day; costs 1 % of the premium per order, at least ₹0.05 a unit; qty 650 per leg.
